03Support Escalations and Incident CommunicationOffer the verified workaround without inventing a fix date+

Your goal

Communicate confirmed impact, temporary relief, unresolved questions, and the next update under pressure.

This lesson in the book, page 22 ↗

A Read the situation

Offer the verified workaround without inventing a fix date

Incident I42 prevents scheduled report exports for eight confirmed customer accounts in the EU region. Support has verified an administrator-only manual download workaround for those accounts. It does not restore automated scheduling. Engineering is investigating; neither a permanent-fix date nor the cause is confirmed. An unsent customer message says the issue will be fixed tomorrow. Customer success manager Asha and support lead Leon must correct it. The next customer update is due at 14:00 UTC, even if the investigation has no new result.

Asha: Customer success manager / Leon: Support lead

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is the confirmed impact?

Answer and explanation

C. Scheduled exports fail for eight confirmed EU-region accounts.

The brief limits confirmed impact to scheduled exports for eight identified accounts.

2. What does the workaround provide?

Answer and explanation

B. Administrator manual downloads, without restored scheduling

The verified workaround is a temporary manual route and does not repair automated scheduling.

3. What is confirmed about timing?

Answer and explanation

B. The next update is at 14:00 UTC.

The communication deadline is confirmed, while the investigation outcome and fix date remain unknown.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Eight confirmed accounts is the known scope, not proof that no others are affected. The manual route provides limited relief to administrators. It does not restore scheduling, establish the root cause, or justify saying the incident has been permanently resolved.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
incident
An event disrupting or degrading a service under the relevant process.coordinate an incident
confirmed impact
The effect established by available evidence.state confirmed impact
affected account
A customer account known to experience the issue.identify affected accounts
scheduled export
Data output arranged to run automatically at specified times.restore scheduled exports
workaround
A temporary way to reduce impact without fully correcting the underlying issue.provide a verified workaround
permanent fix
A correction intended to resolve the underlying defect or cause.validate a permanent fix
reproduction
Recreating reported behavior under specified conditions.reproduce the issue
support case
A tracked customer request or problem report.link the support case
escalation
Routing an issue to a role with appropriate expertise or authority.escalate the case
severity
A classification of impact under an organization's incident rules.assess incident severity
priority
The order or urgency assigned to work.confirm the response priority
incident owner
The person accountable for coordinating the incident response.identify the incident owner
root cause
The underlying cause established through investigation.verify the root cause
mitigation
An action reducing impact or risk.apply an approved mitigation
restoration
Return of a service or capability to the defined working state.confirm service restoration
status update
A communication of current facts and next actions.issue a status update
update cadence
The agreed frequency of status communication.maintain the update cadence
ETA
Estimated time of arrival; also used informally for expected completion of a specified task.qualify an ETA
UTC
Coordinated Universal Time, a shared time reference.specify the time in UTC
known limitation
A confirmed boundary on capability or effectiveness.disclose known limitations
scope of impact
The services, users, or accounts affected.verify the scope of impact
customer impact
The effect on a customer's work or operations.describe customer impact
post-incident review
A later assessment of events, response, and improvements.schedule a post-incident review
resolution criterion
A condition used to determine whether the incident can be closed.confirm resolution criteria

The next-update time and the estimated completion time refer to different events. Asha can promise an update at 14:00 UTC without promising a fix then. Claims about data loss, security, or wider impact require their own verified evidence.

C Notice the language

Certainty is not the only form of reassurance

A customer under pressure needs a usable status, not a confident guess. Acknowledge the disruption and give a specific next contact. Explain unfamiliar technical terms in ordinary language, while keeping the approved workaround and its limits intact.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Acknowledge the impact
I understand that the missing reports are interrupting your daily review.
State the known scope
We have confirmed scheduled-export failures in eight EU-region accounts.
Keep the scope qualified
We are still checking whether other accounts are affected.
Explain the workaround
Administrators can use the verified manual download route.
State the limitation
That route does not restore automated scheduling.
Protect the instructions
Use the approved steps for the affected account configuration.
Correct the promise
We do not have a confirmed permanent-fix date.
Separate two events
Fourteen hundred UTC is the next update time, not a fix deadline.
Maintain contact
We will update you then even if the investigation is still open.
Avoid inventing a cause
The cause has not yet been confirmed.
Coordinate the message
Link the customer case to incident I42 so updates stay consistent.
Check the customer need
Which scheduled report is blocking your immediate work?
Avoid unsafe reassurance
I cannot make a data-loss or security claim without verified information.
Escalate new evidence
Send the new account details through the approved support route.
Define closure
Confirm restoration against the incident team's agreed criteria.
Close with a clear status
A workaround is available; investigation continues; the next update is at fourteen hundred UTC.
6 language and precision notes
Confirmed
Distinguishes verified scope from assumptions about the wider service.
Still checking
Keeps uncertainty visible without implying no investigation is happening.
Does not restore
Makes the temporary route's limitation explicit.
Next update
Promises communication rather than technical completion.
Has not yet
Describes current knowledge without claiming the cause is unknowable.
Approved steps
Avoids improvising technical instructions in a customer conversation.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which corrected message is accurate?

Answer and explanation

B. A verified administrator workaround is available; the fix date remains unconfirmed.

The brief confirms a bounded workaround but does not confirm restoration or a permanent-fix date.

2. What should happen at 14:00 UTC if nothing has changed?

Answer and explanation

B. Send the promised update and state that the investigation continues.

The agreed communication commitment applies even when the technical status is unchanged.

3. Which scope statement avoids overclaiming?

Answer and explanation

B. Eight accounts are confirmed affected; wider impact is still being checked.

Confirmed cases define current knowledge rather than establishing a complete global boundary.

4. Which statement requires additional verified evidence?

Answer and explanation

A. No data was lost and no security issue occurred.

The supplied facts establish neither data-loss status nor security conclusions, so those assurances are unsupported.

Check explanations in the book, page 89 ↗

E Conversations

Offer the verified workaround without inventing a fix date

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Asha: Customer success manager / Leon: Support lead

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • customer impact
  • status update
  • ETA
  • update cadence
  • scope of impact
  • support case
  • resolution criteria
  • workaround
  • permanent fix
  • confirmed impact
  1. Asha
    I am about to send the customer update, but it says fixed tomorrow. Where did that date come from? Has engineering actually confirmed it?
  2. Leon
    No. The 1 is still under investigation. Please remove tomorrow. We can give the verified workaround and the next update time, but we should not manufacture a completion estimate.
  3. Asha
    The customer is missing its morning reports and wants something useful now. What exactly can we offer?
  4. Leon
    The approved 2 lets administrators download the reports manually for the affected configuration. It does not restore scheduled delivery, so we need to explain the manual effort and access requirement.
  5. Asha
    I will avoid saying exports are fully restored. Do we know how many accounts are affected?
  6. Leon
    The 3 is scheduled-export failure in eight accounts in the EU region. That is what we have verified so far, not proof that no additional accounts will report the issue.
  7. Asha
    One contact has asked whether the underlying report data has disappeared. I do not see an approved statement about that.
  8. Leon
    Do not add one without verification. The 4 needs evidence for each claim. We should route the question to the incident team instead of inferring data loss, or its absence, from an export symptom.
  9. Asha
    Our account contact has two different messages: one from the ticket and one from the incident update. How do we get them back to a consistent status?
  10. Leon
    Link the 5 to I42 and use the approved incident status. Preserve their specific business impact, but do not create a separate fix date in the account notes.
  11. Asha
    Their finance lead needs a report before an afternoon meeting. Can I ask which report is most urgent without changing the incident severity myself?
  12. Leon
    Yes. Record the 6 and send it through the response process. The team can assess urgency using the actual facts; a forceful message alone does not define the technical severity.
  13. Asha
    We promised another message at fourteen hundred UTC. If engineering has no new finding, should I wait until there is more to say?
  14. Leon
    Keep the 7. At fourteen hundred UTC, state that investigation continues, confirm any unchanged workaround, and give the next communication point agreed by the response team.
  15. Asha
    I want to be clear that fourteen hundred refers to communication. Otherwise the customer may hear it as another completion time.
  16. Leon
    Exactly. It is not an 8 for a fix. Use the words next update and name UTC, rather than saying we will have it for you by two.
  17. Asha
    If someone can download the report manually, should support close the incident or leave it open?
  18. Leon
    Follow the agreed 9. Temporary relief does not by itself show that scheduling is restored. The incident team needs the defined verification before declaring the affected capability resolved.
  19. Asha
    Then my message will acknowledge the disruption, give the approved manual option, explain its limit, and keep the fix date unconfirmed.
  20. Leon
    Good. Check that the 10 matches those facts and says when we will contact them again. That is a useful commitment we can keep while the technical investigation continues.

Answers and explanations
  1. permanent fix Turn 2. No permanent-fix date is confirmed; the proposed message incorrectly converts uncertainty into a promise.
  2. workaround Turn 4. The workaround allows administrator manual downloads but does not correct automated scheduling or establish permanent resolution.
  3. confirmed impact Turn 6. Confirmed impact currently covers scheduled-export failures for eight identified EU-region accounts, not every account or service.
  4. scope of impact Turn 8. The scope must be verified for each claim; export failure alone establishes neither data loss nor its absence.
  5. support case Turn 10. Linking the support case to I42 keeps account-specific reports connected to the coordinated incident response.
  6. customer impact Turn 12. Customer impact describes the blocked business work and helps reviewers assess needs without inventing a resolution promise.
  7. update cadence Turn 14. The agreed cadence requires the promised update even if the investigation has not produced a new finding.
  8. ETA Turn 16. ETA concerns estimated completion of a specified event; the update appointment is not a technical fix estimate.
  9. resolution criteria Turn 18. Resolution criteria determine closure; successful temporary manual access does not establish restoration of scheduled exports.
  10. status update Turn 20. The status update should align verified impact, workaround limits, unknowns, and the confirmed communication time.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Asha: I am about to send the customer update, but it says fixed tomorrow. Where did that date come from? Has engineering actually confirmed it?
  2. Leon: No. The permanent fix is still under investigation. Please remove tomorrow. We can give the verified workaround and the next update time, but we should not manufacture a completion estimate.
  3. Asha: The customer is missing its morning reports and wants something useful now. What exactly can we offer?
  4. Leon: The approved workaround lets administrators download the reports manually for the affected configuration. It does not restore scheduled delivery, so we need to explain the manual effort and access requirement.
  5. Asha: I will avoid saying exports are fully restored. Do we know how many accounts are affected?
  6. Leon: The confirmed impact is scheduled-export failure in eight accounts in the EU region. That is what we have verified so far, not proof that no additional accounts will report the issue.
  7. Asha: One contact has asked whether the underlying report data has disappeared. I do not see an approved statement about that.
  8. Leon: Do not add one without verification. The scope of impact needs evidence for each claim. We should route the question to the incident team instead of inferring data loss, or its absence, from an export symptom.
  9. Asha: Our account contact has two different messages: one from the ticket and one from the incident update. How do we get them back to a consistent status?
  10. Leon: Link the support case to I42 and use the approved incident status. Preserve their specific business impact, but do not create a separate fix date in the account notes.
  11. Asha: Their finance lead needs a report before an afternoon meeting. Can I ask which report is most urgent without changing the incident severity myself?
  12. Leon: Yes. Record the customer impact and send it through the response process. The team can assess urgency using the actual facts; a forceful message alone does not define the technical severity.
  13. Asha: We promised another message at fourteen hundred UTC. If engineering has no new finding, should I wait until there is more to say?
  14. Leon: Keep the update cadence. At fourteen hundred UTC, state that investigation continues, confirm any unchanged workaround, and give the next communication point agreed by the response team.
  15. Asha: I want to be clear that fourteen hundred refers to communication. Otherwise the customer may hear it as another completion time.
  16. Leon: Exactly. It is not an ETA for a fix. Use the words next update and name UTC, rather than saying we will have it for you by two.
  17. Asha: If someone can download the report manually, should support close the incident or leave it open?
  18. Leon: Follow the agreed resolution criteria. Temporary relief does not by itself show that scheduling is restored. The incident team needs the defined verification before declaring the affected capability resolved.
  19. Asha: Then my message will acknowledge the disruption, give the approved manual option, explain its limit, and keep the fix date unconfirmed.
  20. Leon: Good. Check that the status update matches those facts and says when we will contact them again. That is a useful commitment we can keep while the technical investigation continues.
Dialogue in the book, page 28 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

A verified workaround, not tomorrow's permanent fix

The lesson's issue has a verified workaround but no confirmed permanent-fix date; a draft update promises a fix tomorrow.

  1. Customer success manager: The workaround is verified, but the draft promises a permanent fix tomorrow without a confirmed date.
  2. Support escalation lead: That promise needs correction. We can report the workaround's status and the continuing investigation separately.
  3. Customer success manager: What should I tell the customer about progress without making the workaround sound like complete resolution?
  4. Support escalation lead: State that a workaround is available for the verified case and that permanent-fix timing remains unconfirmed.
  5. Customer success manager: Should I describe the issue as lower severity simply because the workaround exists?
  6. Support escalation lead: Use the actual impact and support process. A workaround alone doesn't establish the appropriate severity classification.
  7. Customer success manager: I'll also keep the SLA question separate rather than imply a service agreement guarantees tomorrow's repair.
  8. Support escalation lead: Good. Ask the account owner to confirm the applicable communication commitments from the actual agreement.
  9. Customer success manager: I'll replace the unsupported date with a precise status and confirm the next communication arrangement.
  10. Support escalation lead: I'll supply verified technical changes as they become available, without treating an estimate as a commitment.
A fix deployed but not yet verified with the customer

Separate fictional case: engineering deployed a candidate fix for a report-export issue; customer-environment verification has not finished.

  1. Support incident lead: Engineering deployed the candidate fix, but verification in the customer's environment is still pending.
  2. Customer operations lead: Then the issue isn't confirmed resolved for our team, even though deployment is complete?
  3. Support incident lead: Correct. Deployment is a completed action; successful customer verification remains a separate step.
  4. Customer operations lead: Can we resume the affected reporting workflow immediately based on that deployment update?
  5. Support incident lead: Let's follow the agreed verification process and current guidance rather than infer readiness from deployment alone.
  6. Customer operations lead: Understood. I can coordinate the customer-side check once the authorized test arrangement is confirmed.
  7. Support incident lead: Thank you. We'll keep the escalation open for the remaining verification and report any unsuccessful result.
  8. Customer operations lead: Please avoid saying fixed in the subject line while the body says we're still checking.
  9. Support incident lead: Agreed. The status will say candidate fix deployed, customer verification pending, with the next action clearly identified.
  10. Customer operations lead: That gives us an accurate update to share internally without overstating service recovery.
The workaround covers exports, not scheduled delivery

Separate fictional case: a verified workaround allows manual report export; scheduled email delivery remains affected and no permanent-fix date is confirmed.

  1. Customer success manager: Manual export works through the verified workaround, but scheduled email delivery remains affected.
  2. Customer reporting lead: Our team relies on scheduled delivery. The update sounded as though all reporting functionality was restored.
  3. Customer success manager: I should state the boundary clearly: export has a workaround; scheduled delivery still lacks a confirmed resolution.
  4. Customer reporting lead: Could we describe manual export as an option while making its additional effort visible?
  5. Customer success manager: Yes. We shouldn't present the workaround as equivalent to your normal workflow or hide the remaining impact.
  6. Customer reporting lead: Do you have a verified date for the permanent fix, or only an investigation status?
  7. Customer success manager: Only the investigation status. I won't promise a date the support team hasn't confirmed.
  8. Customer reporting lead: Then include the affected scheduled-delivery function when you update the escalation and customer impact.
  9. Customer success manager: I will. I'll also confirm our communication arrangement separately from the unresolved repair timing.
  10. Customer reporting lead: Thank you. A scoped workaround update is useful, provided the unresolved part of our workflow stays visible.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Offer the verified workaround without inventing a fix date
  1. Read Asha and Leon aloud, then swap roles. Remove the unsupported tomorrow promise while offering the verified administrator workaround.
  2. Check the ten gaps. Reread the eight-account scope, manual-download limitation, and unresolved data questions.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Correct another premature fix promise

Incident J18 affects automated invoice downloads for five confirmed accounts. An approved manual route is available to administrators. The fix date is unknown. The next update is 11:30 UTC.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Manager: "The known affected-account count is ___."

Answer and explanation

B. five

Five is the confirmed count, not proof that no further accounts could be affected.

2. Customer: "The temporary route requires ___."

Answer and explanation

A. administrator access

The manual route is restricted to administrators under the supplied workaround conditions.

3. Manager: "The permanent-fix date remains ___."

Answer and explanation

C. unknown

No completion date has been confirmed, despite the available temporary route.

4. Customer: "The next update is at ___."

Answer and explanation

A. 11:30 UTC

The stated time is a communication commitment rather than a promise of technical completion.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Lesson 03: Support Escalations and Incident Communication
Original fictional case: Incident I42 prevents scheduled report exports for eight confirmed customer accounts in the EU region. Support has verified an administrator-only manual download workaround for those accounts. It does not restore automated scheduling. Engineering is investigating; neither a permanent-fix date nor the cause is confirmed. An unsent customer message says the issue will be fixed tomorrow. Customer success manager Asha and support lead Leon must correct it. The next customer update is due at 14:00 UTC, even if the investigation has no new result.
Language workshop: Offer the verified workaround without inventing a fix date
Communication goal: Communicate confirmed impact, temporary relief, unresolved questions, and the next update under pressure.
Language guidance: Eight confirmed accounts is the known scope, not proof that no others are affected. The manual route provides limited relief to administrators. It does not restore scheduling, establish the root cause, or justify saying the incident has been permanently resolved.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I understand that the missing reports are interrupting your daily review. / We have confirmed scheduled-export failures in eight EU-region accounts. / We are still checking whether other accounts are affected. / Administrators can use the verified manual download route. / That route does not restore automated scheduling. / Use the approved steps for the affected account configuration. / We do not have a confirmed permanent-fix date. / Fourteen hundred UTC is the next update time, not a fix deadline. / We will update you then even if the investigation is still open. / The cause has not yet been confirmed. / Link the customer case to incident I42 so updates stay consistent. / Which scheduled report is blocking your immediate work? / I cannot make a data-loss or security claim without verified information. / Send the new account details through the approved support route. / Confirm restoration against the incident team's agreed criteria. / A workaround is available; investigation continues; the next update is at fourteen hundred UTC.
Vocabulary: incident: An event disrupting or degrading a service under the relevant process. / confirmed impact: The effect established by available evidence. / affected account: A customer account known to experience the issue. / scheduled export: Data output arranged to run automatically at specified times. / workaround: A temporary way to reduce impact without fully correcting the underlying issue. / permanent fix: A correction intended to resolve the underlying defect or cause. / reproduction: Recreating reported behavior under specified conditions. / support case: A tracked customer request or problem report. / escalation: Routing an issue to a role with appropriate expertise or authority. / severity: A classification of impact under an organization's incident rules. / priority: The order or urgency assigned to work. / incident owner: The person accountable for coordinating the incident response. / root cause: The underlying cause established through investigation. / mitigation: An action reducing impact or risk. / restoration: Return of a service or capability to the defined working state. / status update: A communication of current facts and next actions. / update cadence: The agreed frequency of status communication. / ETA: Estimated time of arrival; also used informally for expected completion of a specified task. / UTC: Coordinated Universal Time, a shared time reference. / known limitation: A confirmed boundary on capability or effectiveness. / scope of impact: The services, users, or accounts affected. / customer impact: The effect on a customer's work or operations. / post-incident review: A later assessment of events, response, and improvements. / resolution criterion: A condition used to determine whether the incident can be closed.
Speaking task: Read Asha and Leon aloud, then swap roles. Remove the unsupported tomorrow promise while offering the verified administrator workaround. Check the ten gaps. Reread the eight-account scope, manual-download limitation, and unresolved data questions. Give the corrected update again with fourteen-hundred UTC clearly identified as communication timing, not a technical completion estimate.
Partner: Asha: Customer success manager / Leon: Support lead
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Incident J18 affects automated invoice downloads for five confirmed accounts. An approved manual route is available to administrators. The fix date is unknown. The next update is 11:30 UTC.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Asha: If someone can download the report manually, should support close the incident or leave it open?
Leon: Follow the agreed resolution criteria. Temporary relief does not by itself show that scheduling is restored. The incident team needs the defined verification before declaring the affected capability resolved.
Asha: Then my message will acknowledge the disruption, give the approved manual option, explain its limit, and keep the fix date unconfirmed.
Leon: Good. Check that the status update matches those facts and says when we will contact them again. That is a useful commitment we can keep while the technical investigation continues.
END REFERENCE

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning

5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

04QBRs and Business OutcomesReplace the impressive ROI claim with a defensible value story+

Your goal

Present measured change, disclose valuation assumptions, and separate released capacity from proven financial return.

This lesson in the book, page 31 ↗

A Read the situation

Replace the impressive ROI claim with a defensible value story

For a quarterly business review, Kira prepares a slide claiming proven savings and return on investment. Customer finance lead Joel confirms logs showing 40 staff hours for one reporting cycle before implementation and 28 for one cycle afterward. Other process changes occurred too. Kira values the 12-hour difference at an unvalidated $40 per hour and annualizes it over 12 months. The annual subscription costs $3,600; implementation and training costs are not fully recorded. Staffing expenditure has not changed. The slide must distinguish observations, assumptions, attribution, and missing costs.

Kira: Customer success manager / Joel: Customer finance lead

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What change do the time logs show?

Answer and explanation

C. A 12-hour difference between the two recorded cycles

Forty minus twenty-eight is twelve hours, but the logs cover only the stated cycles.

2. Which input has not been validated?

Answer and explanation

A. The $40 hourly valuation

Joel confirms the time logs, while the hourly monetary value remains an assumption.

3. Why is a proven full ROI claim unsupported?

Answer and explanation

D. Attribution and recurring benefits are unverified, and relevant costs are incomplete.

The evidence leaves causal, recurring-benefit, valuation, and cost gaps that prevent the asserted financial conclusion.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The observed difference is 40 minus 28, or 12 hours. At the assumed $40 per hour, that equals $480 for a cycle. Multiplying by 12 gives $5,760 only if the monthly frequency, repeated difference, and valuation assumptions hold.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
QBR
Quarterly business review; a periodic discussion of outcomes, priorities, and progress.prepare a QBR
business outcome
A result relevant to the customer's goals or operations.validate business outcomes
baseline
The defined starting measure for a comparison.establish a baseline
comparison period
The time window used as the counterpart in an analysis.align comparison periods
observed change
A measured difference, without automatically establishing its cause.report observed change
attribution
Assigning an effect to a cause or contributor.qualify attribution
time saving
A reduction in time required under a stated comparison.validate time savings
released capacity
Staff time made available for other work.describe released capacity
cash saving
A reduction in actual expenditure under a defined comparison.verify cash savings
hourly valuation
The monetary value assigned to an hour for an analysis.validate the hourly valuation
annualization
Extending a shorter-period amount to an annual estimate using assumptions.disclose annualization assumptions
benefit estimate
A calculated or proposed value not necessarily realized.qualify the benefit estimate
ROI
Return on investment; net benefit relative to investment cost on a stated basis.substantiate ROI
net benefit
Benefits minus included costs under a defined calculation.calculate net benefit
cost base
The costs included in a comparison or calculation.define the cost base
implementation cost
Expenditure or resources used to introduce the solution.capture implementation costs
training cost
The resources or expenditure associated with preparing users.include training costs
subscription cost
The recurring access charge under the relevant agreement.confirm subscription costs
validation
Checking a claim or measure against appropriate evidence.obtain customer validation
value realization
Actual achievement of an intended customer benefit.track value realization
confounding factor
Another influence that complicates attribution of an observed change.identify confounding factors
sensitivity analysis
Examining how results change when assumptions vary.test assumption sensitivity
outcome owner
The person responsible for confirming and tracking the relevant result.name the outcome owner
value narrative
An explanation connecting evidence to a customer-relevant benefit.build a credible value narrative

A common ROI expression is (benefits minus costs) divided by costs, using a defined period and consistent basis. Here neither a complete cost base nor validated recurring monetary benefit is available. Released time is not automatically reduced payroll or cash expenditure.

C Notice the language

A challenge to the numbers can strengthen the relationship

A customer questioning a value slide is not necessarily rejecting the product. Welcome the distinction between useful operational improvement and an unsupported financial claim. Agree which figures the customer validates, which remain assumptions, and what evidence the next review needs.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Acknowledge the correction
You are right to distinguish the time logs from a proven financial return.
State the observation
The recorded cycle fell from forty staff hours to twenty-eight.
Calculate the difference
That is twelve fewer hours in the compared cycles.
Limit the inference
The comparison does not isolate the product's contribution.
Identify another influence
Other process changes occurred during the same period.
Label the valuation
Forty dollars per hour is an assumption that needs your validation.
State the conditional figure
At that rate, twelve hours would be valued at four hundred eighty dollars.
Qualify annualization
The annual estimate assumes the same benefit in twelve monthly cycles.
Distinguish capacity
The time difference may release capacity without reducing cash expenditure.
Expose missing costs
Implementation and training costs are not fully recorded.
Withdraw the overclaim
We should not call this proven ROI.
Keep the useful result
The operational change is still worth reporting accurately.
Ask for ownership
Who can validate the time measure and the monetary basis?
Agree the next evidence
Compare further cycles using a consistent task definition.
Separate evidence and assumption
Let us label each input before discussing the result.
Close the review
We will report the observed change and a plan to validate value over time.
6 language and precision notes
Recorded
Grounds the claim in the actual time logs.
Compared cycles
Avoids presenting a limited observation as a permanent monthly result.
At that rate
Makes the monetary figure conditional on the valuation.
Assumes
Identifies an input that has not been demonstrated.
May release
Keeps potential capacity distinct from verified cash savings.
Still worth reporting
Corrects an overclaim without dismissing a useful operational signal.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. What follows from the stated hourly assumption?

Answer and explanation

C. Twelve hours at $40 would be valued at $480 per cycle.

Multiplication produces a conditional value, not proof of cash savings or exclusive causation.

2. What is required to describe $5,760 responsibly?

Answer and explanation

A. State the assumptions of twelve similar monthly cycles and the unvalidated hourly value.

Annualization extends a limited observation and therefore needs explicit frequency, recurrence, and valuation assumptions.

3. Which cost statement is accurate?

Answer and explanation

C. The $3,600 subscription is known, but the full cost base is incomplete.

Incomplete cost records prevent a full return calculation without establishing either a positive or negative result.

4. Which review conclusion is strongest?

Answer and explanation

C. Report the observed operational change and agree a consistent validation plan.

A bounded observation and explicit next evidence preserve useful learning without inventing financial certainty.

E Conversations

Replace the impressive ROI claim with a defensible value story

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Kira: Customer success manager / Joel: Customer finance lead

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • observed change
  • hourly valuation
  • cost base
  • attribution
  • value narrative
  • outcome owner
  • baseline
  • annualization
  • value realization
  • released capacity
  1. Joel
    I signed off the time logs, not a proven-ROI claim. Can we go through the slide before this reaches the quarterly review?
  2. Kira
    You are right. The 1 is from forty staff hours to twenty-eight for the two recorded cycles. I have moved too quickly from that difference to a broader claim about return.
  3. Joel
    There were other process changes at the same time. We simplified the approval route as well as introducing the product.
  4. Kira
    Then 2 needs qualification. The comparison does not isolate what the product caused. We can report the change without claiming that every hour came from one feature or supplier.
  5. Joel
    The twelve-hour difference is correct. Where did the forty-dollar hourly number come from?
  6. Kira
    It is an unvalidated 3 in my calculation. At that rate, twelve hours would be worth four hundred eighty dollars for a cycle, but I should not present your approval as already given.
  7. Joel
    And the five-thousand-seven-hundred-sixty-dollar annual figure assumes that the same thing happens every month?
  8. Kira
    Yes. That 4 assumes twelve monthly cycles with the same difference and the same valuation. We have one before-and-after comparison, not twelve months of validated recurring benefits.
  9. Joel
    Nobody's pay has changed, and we have not reduced the team. The time may be useful, but it has not disappeared from our expenditure.
  10. Kira
    Then 5 is the relevant distinction. Staff may use the time for other work. Calling it a cash saving would imply a spending reduction that you have not reported.
  11. Joel
    We pay thirty-six hundred annually for the subscription. The implementation effort and training time are not fully in the model yet.
  12. Kira
    The 6 is incomplete. A return calculation needs the relevant benefits and costs on a consistent basis and over a defined period, not only the easiest numbers to find.
  13. Joel
    I am not saying there is no value. The reporting cycle took less time. I want that useful result kept, without turning it into money we have not saved.
  14. Kira
    Agreed. We can build a stronger 7 around what we know. The recorded change is useful; the mistake was presenting an assumed financial interpretation as an established result.
  15. Joel
    For the next review, let us measure the same reporting task across more cycles. Otherwise changes in the task itself could distort the comparison.
  16. Kira
    We will keep the 8 and task definition explicit. We should also record relevant process changes and exceptions rather than quietly treating unlike cycles as equivalent.
  17. Joel
    I can validate the finance inputs, while our operations lead confirms the time records and what people actually do with the released hours.
  18. Kira
    Naming an 9 for each measure will help. Your finance validation and the operations evidence answer different questions, so neither should be substituted for the other.
  19. Joel
    Please revise the slide before the business review. Keep the time difference, but remove the proven-return headline.
  20. Kira
    I will describe 10 only where we have evidence. The revised slide will show the observed twelve-hour difference, label the assumptions, identify missing costs, and state the next validation steps.

Answers and explanations
  1. observed change Turn 2. The logs establish a twelve-hour difference between two cycles, not a proven recurring financial return.
  2. attribution Turn 4. Attribution remains uncertain because other process changes could contribute to the observed time difference.
  3. hourly valuation Turn 6. The forty-dollar hourly valuation is an assumption, not a rate already confirmed by the customer.
  4. annualization Turn 8. Annualization multiplies the conditional cycle value by twelve, assuming similar monthly results rather than demonstrating them.
  5. released capacity Turn 10. Released capacity makes time available for other work without necessarily lowering payroll or cash expenditure.
  6. cost base Turn 12. The cost base is incomplete because known subscription charges exclude unrecorded implementation and training resources.
  7. value narrative Turn 14. A value narrative can report useful operational evidence while clearly separating assumptions and missing financial inputs.
  8. baseline Turn 16. The baseline must use a consistent task and measurement definition so later comparisons remain interpretable.
  9. outcome owner Turn 18. The outcome owner confirms the relevant result; finance and operations can validate different parts of the evidence.
  10. value realization Turn 20. Value realization concerns benefits actually achieved, which must be separated from the book's conditional financial estimates.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Joel: I signed off the time logs, not a proven-ROI claim. Can we go through the slide before this reaches the quarterly review?
  2. Kira: You are right. The observed change is from forty staff hours to twenty-eight for the two recorded cycles. I have moved too quickly from that difference to a broader claim about return.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Estimated savings do not establish validated ROI

The lesson's business review claims substantial return on investment from estimated time savings the customer has not validated.

  1. Customer success manager: The QBR draft claims a large return on investment, but its time-saving estimates haven't been validated by your team.
  2. Customer executive sponsor: Then we shouldn't present the figure as a measured business outcome. What evidence does it actually reflect?
  3. Customer success manager: It reflects assumptions about time saved, not a confirmed customer measurement of those savings.
  4. Customer executive sponsor: Could we keep the estimate if its assumptions and limitations are clearly visible?
  5. Customer success manager: We can discuss it as an estimate, subject to the review's purpose, without labeling it realized ROI.
  6. Customer executive sponsor: Please explain ROI as return on investment and identify which costs the calculation includes or leaves unresolved.
  7. Customer success manager: Agreed. We should not convert an unverified time estimate into a financial claim without that context.
  8. Customer executive sponsor: Let's review the measurement approach before using the figure in an executive decision.
  9. Customer success manager: I'll distinguish observed information, estimated savings, and missing cost inputs in the quarterly business review.
  10. Customer executive sponsor: That lets us discuss potential value without treating an unvalidated model as an achieved result.
A pilot outcome outside the full account scope

Separate fictional case: a pilot team reduced measured report preparation from four hours to three; no other teams have been measured.

  1. Customer account director: The pilot team reduced report preparation from four hours to three. Can the QBR claim this across the account?
  2. Customer analytics lead: Only the pilot team was measured. We don't have evidence that other teams achieved the same change.
  3. Customer account director: Then the observed business outcome is limited to that team's measured reporting task.
  4. Customer analytics lead: Correct. We should also preserve the measurement conditions rather than imply every report takes one hour less.
  5. Customer account director: Could we show an account-wide projection as a separate scenario rather than a realized saving?
  6. Customer analytics lead: Only with its assumptions explicit and suitable review. It must not look like measured account performance.
  7. Customer account director: I'll lead with the actual pilot result and label broader potential as untested.
  8. Customer analytics lead: Good. The executive sponsor needs to know which claim is observation and which is extrapolation.
  9. Customer account director: Let's agree what additional team evidence would support a wider business-outcome discussion.
  10. Customer analytics lead: I'll identify the measurement gaps before we put any account-wide ROI claim into the readout.
Time released is not automatically payroll saved

Separate fictional case: a customer validates reduced time on a reporting task but has not measured any reduction in payroll or other expenditure.

  1. Customer success director: Your team validated less time spent on reporting. Should the QBR translate that directly into payroll savings?
  2. Customer finance lead: No. We haven't measured lower payroll or other expenditure, so that would overstate the financial outcome.
  3. Customer success director: Then released time is the observed benefit, while an actual cash saving remains unestablished.
  4. Customer finance lead: Exactly. Staff may use that time for other work, but we haven't measured that use either.
  5. Customer success director: I'll avoid claiming either reduced spending or additional output without evidence for those next steps.
  6. Customer finance lead: Good. A monetary scenario would need clear assumptions and costs, separate from the validated time result.
  7. Customer success director: Would you review the wording before we present it to the executive sponsor?
  8. Customer finance lead: Yes. Keep the measured task, time boundary, and unconfirmed financial interpretation visible.
  9. Customer success director: I'll describe the business outcome as reduced reporting time, with no claim of realized payroll savings.
  10. Customer finance lead: That is accurate and still meaningful. We can discuss any further value analysis as a separate next step.

Explain another conditional value estimate

A team records 30 hours before and 24 hours after a change for one cycle. An unvalidated rate of $35 per hour is used. Staffing expenditure is unchanged, and implementation costs are missing.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Customer: "The observed time difference is ___."

Answer and explanation

B. six hours

Thirty minus twenty-four gives six hours for the compared cycle, not a proven recurring annual benefit.

2. Manager: "At the assumed rate, the cycle value would be ___."

Answer and explanation

B. $210

Six multiplied by thirty-five equals two hundred ten, conditional on the unvalidated hourly value.

3. Customer: "Unchanged expenditure means this is not verified ___."

Answer and explanation

A. cash savings

Time released does not establish a reduction in actual spending.

4. Manager: "A full return calculation still lacks ___."

Answer and explanation

A. implementation costs

The missing implementation costs make the relevant cost base incomplete.

Vocabulary: QBR: Quarterly business review; a periodic discussion of outcomes, priorities, and progress. / business outcome: A result relevant to the customer's goals or operations. / baseline: The defined starting measure for a comparison. / comparison period: The time window used as the counterpart in an analysis. / observed change: A measured difference, without automatically establishing its cause. / attribution: Assigning an effect to a cause or contributor. / time saving: A reduction in time required under a stated comparison. / released capacity: Staff time made available for other work. / cash saving: A reduction in actual expenditure under a defined comparison. / hourly valuation: The monetary value assigned to an hour for an analysis. / annualization: Extending a shorter-period amount to an annual estimate using assumptions. / benefit estimate: A calculated or proposed value not necessarily realized. / ROI: Return on investment; net benefit relative to investment cost on a stated basis. / net benefit: Benefits minus included costs under a defined calculation. / cost base: The costs included in a comparison or calculation. / implementation cost: Expenditure or resources used to introduce the solution. / training cost: The resources or expenditure associated with preparing users. / subscription cost: The recurring access charge under the relevant agreement. / validation: Checking a claim or measure against appropriate evidence. / value realization: Actual achievement of an intended customer benefit. / confounding factor: Another influence that complicates attribution of an observed change. / sensitivity analysis: Examining how results change when assumptions vary. / outcome owner: The person responsible for confirming and tracking the relevant result. / value narrative: An explanation connecting evidence to a customer-relevant benefit.
Speaking task: Read Joel and Kira aloud, then swap roles. State the twelve-hour observed difference before its conditional monetary valuation. Check all ten gaps. Reread the forty-dollar assumption, twelve-cycle annualization, and incomplete cost base. Repeat the corrected review conclusion with the operational improvement intact and the proven-return headline withdrawn.
Partner: Kira: Customer success manager / Joel: Customer finance lead
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: A team records 30 hours before and 24 hours after a change for one cycle. An unvalidated rate of $35 per hour is used. Staffing expenditure is unchanged, and implementation costs are missing.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Joel: I can validate the finance inputs, while our operations lead confirms the time records and what people actually do with the released hours.
Kira: Naming an outcome owner for each measure will help. Your finance validation and the operations evidence answer different questions, so neither should be substituted for the other.
Joel: Please revise the slide before the business review. Keep the time difference, but remove the proven-return headline.
Kira: I will describe value realization only where we have evidence. The revised slide will show the observed twelve-hour difference, label the assumptions, identify missing costs, and state the next validation steps.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

Lesson 04: QBRs and Business Outcomes
Original fictional case: For a quarterly business review, Kira prepares a slide claiming proven savings and return on investment. Kira values the 12-hour difference at an unvalidated $40 per hour and annualizes it over 12 months. The annual subscription costs $3,600; implementation and training costs are not fully recorded. Staffing expenditure has not changed. The slide must distinguish observations, assumptions, attribution, and missing costs.

05Renewals and Churn RiskRebuild the renewal conversation after the main contact leaves+

Your goal

Explore changed priorities, verify decision roles, and describe renewal risk without treating it as a verdict.

This lesson in the book, page 40 ↗

A Read the situation

Rebuild the renewal conversation after the main contact leaves

Account R31 has a $60,000 annual recurring subscription scheduled for renewal on 12 November, six weeks away. Its main contact has left. New operations contact Ella has not confirmed current priorities or who owns the renewal decision and budget. Earlier reports focused on reducing a service backlog, but the business is now reorganizing. Customer success manager Rafael has no cancellation notice and no renewal approval. Ella agrees to a discovery meeting on 8 October at 10:00 UTC. Actual notice terms and the purchasing route still need checking.

Ella: New customer operations contact / Rafael: Customer success manager

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is the renewal date?

Answer and explanation

D. 12 November

The subscription is scheduled for renewal on 12 November; 8 October is a discovery meeting.

2. What is known about the decision?

Answer and explanation

D. Neither cancellation nor renewal approval has been received.

The brief leaves the commercial outcome open while identifying information gaps and a stakeholder change.

3. What is the next agreed customer event?

Answer and explanation

A. A discovery meeting on 8 October at 10:00 UTC

The agreed meeting explores the current position and is not itself an approval or signature.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

A contact leaving is a risk signal, not a cancellation. An agreed meeting is progress, not renewal approval. The $60,000 recurring amount does not include an invented expansion or establish recognized revenue. Verify dates and notice terms in the actual agreement.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
renewal
Continuation of an agreement or subscription under applicable terms.coordinate a renewal
renewal date
The date specified for the relevant renewal event.verify the renewal date
renewal owner
The role coordinating or responsible for the renewal decision process.identify the renewal owner
budget owner
The person accountable for the relevant funding decision.confirm the budget owner
stakeholder change
A change in people involved in or influencing the relationship.track stakeholder changes
executive sponsor
A senior person supporting the initiative within the customer organization.reestablish executive sponsorship
champion
An internal advocate who actively helps the initiative progress.verify the champion relationship
reorganization
A change in organizational structure, roles, or responsibilities.understand reorganization effects
churn risk
The possibility of losing customer business, with its basis specified.assess churn risk
logo churn
Loss of customer accounts, distinct from the amount of revenue lost.measure logo churn
revenue churn
Recurring revenue lost under a specified measurement definition.distinguish revenue churn
contraction
A reduction in business from an existing customer.identify contraction risk
retention
Continuation of customer business under a stated measure.improve customer retention
GRR
Gross revenue retention; retained starting recurring revenue excluding expansion on a defined basis.calculate GRR
NRR
Net revenue retention; retained starting recurring revenue including expansion on a defined basis.calculate NRR
ARR
Annual recurring revenue, measured under the organization's stated inclusion rules.verify ARR
risk signal
Evidence that may indicate a potential problem, not a certain outcome.record a risk signal
save plan
A defined set of actions addressing an evidenced retention risk.agree a save plan
renewal forecast
An estimate of renewal outcomes based on current evidence.update the renewal forecast
notice provision
A contract term concerning required notices and their conditions.review notice provisions
procurement route
The customer's process for reviewing and completing a purchase.verify the procurement route
value evidence
Information supporting an actual customer benefit.refresh value evidence
success criterion
A condition used to judge achievement of a desired result.reconfirm success criteria
renewal plan
A shared sequence of renewal actions, owners, and dates.maintain the renewal plan

For a defined starting cohort, GRR excludes expansion while NRR includes it. If starting recurring revenue is 100, losses are 10, contraction is 5, and expansion is 20, GRR is 85% and NRR is 105%, using the same basis.

C Notice the language

Do not make a new contact inherit old enthusiasm

A replacement stakeholder may have different priorities or knowledge from the previous contact. Give a brief factual handover and invite correction. A change in sponsorship is a reason to learn more, not permission to pressure the new person into endorsing an earlier plan.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Acknowledge the transition
I understand that responsibilities have changed since our last review.
Avoid inherited assumptions
I do not want to assume the previous priorities are still yours.
Give a factual handover
Earlier discussions focused on reducing the service backlog.
Ask about the present
Which outcomes matter most during the reorganization?
Confirm the date
The recorded renewal date is the twelfth of November.
Separate status from risk
We have neither a cancellation notice nor renewal approval.
Ask about ownership
Who now coordinates the renewal decision?
Check funding separately
Who owns the relevant budget?
Respect the new contact
How would you prefer us to involve the other decision participants?
Verify the process
We need the current purchasing route and required steps.
Check contractual details
Let us verify the actual notice provisions rather than assume them.
Use evidence
Which results can your team validate from the last period?
Avoid premature discounting
We should understand the issue before proposing a commercial change.
Make the next meeting useful
Let us use the eighth of October to confirm priorities and decision roles.
Keep the forecast honest
The outcome remains unconfirmed while those gaps are open.
Close the plan
Record each action, owner, evidence source, and next date.
6 language and precision notes
Still yours
Invites the new stakeholder to confirm or change earlier priorities.
Recorded
Identifies the current document information without inventing revised terms.
Neither ... nor
Keeps both unsupported conclusions out of the status report.
Now
Recognizes that decision roles may have changed.
Before proposing
Sequences diagnosis ahead of a commercial response.
Unconfirmed
Avoids disguising uncertainty as either retention or loss.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which internal renewal update is justified?

Answer and explanation

A. The contact has changed; priorities and decision roles need confirmation; the outcome is open.

The known stakeholder change supports investigation, not a predetermined commercial outcome.

2. What should Rafael ask Ella first?

Answer and explanation

A. What matters now and who participates in the decision

Open factual discovery establishes the current context without imposing an unsupported interpretation.

3. For starting recurring revenue 100, loss 10, contraction 5, and expansion 20, what is GRR?

Answer and explanation

B. 85%

Gross retention excludes expansion: one hundred minus ten minus five, divided by one hundred.

4. Using those same figures, what is NRR?

Answer and explanation

A. 105%

Net retention includes expansion: one hundred minus ten minus five plus twenty, divided by one hundred.

Rebuild the renewal conversation after the main contact leaves

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Ella: New customer operations contact / Rafael: Customer success manager

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Answers and explanations
  1. renewal owner Turn 2. The renewal owner coordinates the decision process; Ella has not confirmed that this responsibility belongs to her.
  2. success criteria Turn 4. Success criteria need reconfirmation because the reorganization may change which customer outcomes matter most.
  3. budget owner Turn 6. The budget owner controls the relevant funding decision; an operations role or meeting invitation does not establish that authority.
  4. stakeholder change Turn 8. The departure and replacement of the main contact create an information gap, not automatic cancellation or renewal.
  5. value evidence Turn 10. Value evidence should show validated customer results, with uncertain or outdated claims clearly identified.
  6. risk signal Turn 12. A risk signal indicates a possible retention issue but does not establish the customer's final decision.
  7. procurement route Turn 14. The procurement route identifies the actual customer purchasing steps, rather than assuming operations can complete them alone.
  8. save plan Turn 16. A save plan should address an evidenced retention problem; an unexplained discount may miss the actual customer issue.
  9. renewal forecast Turn 18. The renewal forecast must reflect current evidence; a booked meeting is not approval or a guaranteed outcome.
  10. renewal plan Turn 20. The renewal plan records the agreed actions, owners, and dates while preserving unresolved decision questions.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Six weeks to renewal, new priorities unknown

The lesson's renewal is six weeks away; the main contact has changed and current customer priorities are unconfirmed.

  1. Customer success manager: Your renewal is six weeks away. Could we arrange a discussion with the new owner to confirm current priorities?
  2. Customer account coordinator: Yes. The main contact changed, and the previous success plan may not reflect the new owner's needs.
  3. Customer success manager: Please identify who can discuss the business priorities and who handles the commercial terms.
  4. Customer account coordinator: I'll check those roles rather than assume the new contact controls both decisions.
  5. Customer success manager: Thank you. We need to understand any value gap before discussing a renewal proposal as though nothing changed.
  6. Customer account coordinator: Does the contact change mean you consider us likely to leave, even without hearing our current plans?
  7. Customer success manager: No. It creates a need for clarification, not proof of churn. Our assessment should reflect actual evidence.
  8. Customer account coordinator: I'll seek an appropriate meeting time and tell you who can attend.
  9. Customer success manager: I'll bring the existing goals and ask what remains relevant, without assuming renewed commitment.
  10. Customer account coordinator: That will give the new owner a practical conversation rather than a request to endorse the old plan unchanged.
A renewal conversation needs the service owner

Separate fictional case: a procurement contact requests renewal pricing but has no information about whether the service team still uses the purchased workflow.

  1. Renewal manager: Could we involve the service owner before finalizing the renewal discussion? Current workflow use and priorities remain unclear.
  2. Customer procurement officer: I can request pricing, but I don't know whether the service team still needs the same scope.
  3. Renewal manager: Exactly. Commercial terms need to reflect the actual requirement rather than simply repeat last year's order.
  4. Customer procurement officer: Would you like me to ask the service owner for current use cases and any unmet needs?
  5. Renewal manager: Yes. That helps clarify a possible value gap without assuming dissatisfaction or expansion needs.
  6. Customer procurement officer: I can't promise their availability today, but I can ask and return with a realistic meeting option.
  7. Renewal manager: Thank you. Please also confirm who will approve any scope change, so the review reaches the right people.
  8. Customer procurement officer: I'll clarify that separately from my role coordinating the quote.
  9. Renewal manager: I'll keep the renewal status factual and avoid treating a pricing request as acceptance of the same service package.
  10. Customer procurement officer: Agreed. Let's establish the requirement first, then discuss the appropriate commercial proposal.
A competitor inquiry is a clarification opportunity

Separate fictional case: a customer mentions reviewing another supplier, but has not stated an intention to cancel or explained the comparison criteria.

  1. Customer success manager: You mentioned reviewing another supplier. Could we discuss which needs or gaps you want the comparison to address?
  2. Customer service director: We are exploring options, not announcing cancellation. We haven't shared our criteria with you yet.
  3. Customer success manager: Thank you for clarifying. I won't record the inquiry as a confirmed decision to leave.
  4. Customer service director: Could we focus on our current workflow concerns before discussing discounts or commercial terms?
  5. Customer success manager: Absolutely. Please identify the main value gap and who should join a focused review.
  6. Customer service director: I'll ask the workflow owner to bring specific examples and confirm a suitable time.
  7. Customer success manager: Good. We can distinguish issues we can investigate from outcomes we cannot promise.
  8. Customer service director: I appreciate that. We don't need an immediate assurance that every requirement will be satisfied.
  9. Customer success manager: I'll arrange the review and keep churn risk as an assessment with evidence, not a prediction presented as fact.
  10. Customer service director: Let's use the discussion to clarify our priorities before either side assumes the renewal outcome.

  2. Check the ten gaps. Reread the distinct operational, budget, procurement, and renewal-owner roles.

Describe another renewal without guessing the outcome

Account T08 renews on 20 December. Its sponsor has moved roles. New contact Hugo agrees to a needs review on 4 November but has not confirmed the budget owner. No renewal or cancellation decision is recorded.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Manager: "The recorded renewal date is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. 20 December

The renewal date differs from the earlier needs-review meeting.

2. Contact: "The fourth of November is a ___."

Answer and explanation

B. needs review

The agreed event explores needs and is not a contract signature or approval.

3. Manager: "The funding decision role remains ___."

Answer and explanation

B. unconfirmed

The new contact has not identified the budget owner.

4. Contact: "The commercial outcome is still ___."

Answer and explanation

B. open

Neither renewal nor cancellation is recorded, so neither conclusion is justified.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary

2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.
